## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T21:07:49.177Z

I'm puzzling over why the worker status remained "working" after the agent's turn ended at 20:47:01 — the final response had origin "task-notification," suggesting the main turn for the 20:16 request ended earlier, possibly because the agent launched background subagents and the original turn closed out before those finished.

My theory now: at 20:44 and 20:47, task-notification events may have triggered spontaneous turns outside the original ask, and if the bridge marks the worker "working" when such a turn starts but nothing persists "idle" when it ends (since no ask is awaiting it), the worker status would stick at "working" in the DB until the reaper fires five minutes later. I also want to check whether liveAgent was actually quiesced — the worker_session_resumed event implies resumeMissingDirectWorker ran, meaning liveAgentQuiesced was false, so either the bridge also failed to detect the turn ending or the liveAgent was missing entirely, triggering the cancel-and-resume-and-redeliver sequence. I should verify this by checking the usage entry timestamps and origins across that 20:16–20:47 window along with the bridge logs, and give a status update before digging further.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T21:08:17.638Z

I'm tracing the timeline: the human ask ended at 20:17:43 with background subagents launched, then spontaneous task-notification turns fired at 20:22, 20:24, 20:33, and 20:47. I'm checking whether the worker status flipped to idle after the original ask, got set back to "working" by those spontaneous turns, and never got reset afterward.

That lines up with the reaper triggering at 20:52 — it found the worker idle for 301 seconds since the 20:47:01 entry but still marked "working" with no quiesced liveAgent, so it canceled and redelivered the last user message from 20:16, even though that message was already answered at 20:17:43. The reaper's own comment claims redelivery is always safe since "a duplicate response is recoverable," but that's the actual design flaw — redelivering an already-answered message to a stateful agent isn't harmless, since the agent treats it as a brand-new instruction. I want to pull the bridge or controller logs around 20:47–20:52 to confirm this sequence for that worker.

/** Codex reports `_meta.codex.threadStatus` on every turn boundary, prompted or not. */
function readProviderThreadStatus(update: unknown): "active" | "idle" | null {
  const meta = asRecord(asRecord(update)?._meta);
  const status = asRecord(asRecord(meta?.codex)?.threadStatus)?.type;
  return status === "active" || status === "idle" ? status : null;
}

/**
 * Track turns the provider runs without a prompt from us. A Codex /goal keeps
 * starting turns after the prompted one ends; without this the agent read as
 * idle (or working forever) and the runner settled a conversation that was
 * still producing output.
 */
function applyProviderThreadStatus(record: AgentRecord, status: "active" | "idle" | null) {
  if (!status) return;
  record.providerTurnActive = status === "active";
  // `askAgent` settles the turn it started; it reads `providerTurnActive` then.
  if (record.promptInFlight) return;
  if (status === "active" && record.state === "idle") {
    record.state = "working";
    record.lastText = record.currentText || record.lastText;
    record.currentText = "";
    record.activeOutputEntryId = null;
    record.stopReason = null;
    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "acp.provider_turn_started", workerId: record.name });
  } else if (status === "idle" && record.state === "working") {
    record.lastText = record.currentText || record.lastText;
    record.currentText = "";
    record.stopReason = "end_turn";
    record.state = "idle";
    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "acp.provider_turn_ended", workerId: record.name });
  }
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T21:09:02.941Z

I'm tracing the first root cause around the bridge: for Claude's unprompted turns, a tool call or permission request flips the record state to "working," but nothing resets it to idle when that turn finishes since Claude lacks a threadStatus like Codex does—its actual end-of-turn signal is the usage_update event carrying the final cost.

That leaves the bridge stuck "working" after the 20:33–20:47 turn (which likely ran tools), so the DB sync stays "working" too, and five minutes later the reaper sees liveAgentQuiesced=false, cancels, resumes, and redelivers the last user input. The second root cause is that reaper redelivery itself: it resends the last user message even though it was already answered in the 20:17:43 human turn, and the code comment's assumption that "a duplicate response is recoverable" doesn't hold for agents with side effects—turning a stale status flag into unauthorized execution. I also want to double check whether the 20:33–20:47 turn actually had tool_calls and confirm the DB status, so I'm going to look at entries 1198–1415 to verify their types.

This confirms Claude lacks a threadStatus field, so unprompted tool-running turns leave state stuck at "working" indefinitely, with turn-end only signaled by the usage_update carrying the final cost. Given the user's angry bug report implies a fix request, and OmniHarness's direct-control instructions treat a request for an outcome as authorization to act, I'm leaning toward fixing it even though they didn't explicitly say so—though their complaint about the harness making the agent do things it wasn't told to is worth weighing carefully.

I'm torn: the irony is that the user's rage is specifically about unauthorized implementation, so diagnosing and proposing a fix rather than just implementing it feels safer here, since intent isn't fully explicit. I also want to check what the rogue agent actually did in cloudcaptions/directorscut between 20:52 and 21:05, so I should scan the tool calls in that window to summarize which files changed.

## Claude · 2026-10-02T21:09:34.543Z

You're right: you never sent that message twice. OmniHarness's stuck-worker reaper replayed it, and there was no model switch either.

**What happened in run `3d628f9b568a`**

| Time | Event |
|---|---|
| 20:16:32 | You send "web too why not… yes go ahead with plan" |
| 20:17:43 | That turn ends. The agent had started background subagents. |
| 20:22, 20:24, 20:33, 20:47 | Each time a background task finished, Claude started a new turn on its own. The last one ends at 20:47:01 with "Say the word and I'll start on Task 0." |
| 20:52:07–09 | The reaper decides the worker is stuck. It cancels and resumes the session, then sends your 20:16 message again (`recovery_continuation_started`, `redeliveredMessageId: 8d9ccc72…`, `idleSeconds: 301`). |
| 20:52:14 | The agent sees the same message twice and makes up a "model switch" to explain it. Model config was `opus[1m]` both before and after. Then it starts implementing. |
| 21:05:00 | Your "STOP IMPLEMENTING" gets through. |

**Two bugs together caused this**

1. **The worker state never goes back to idle after a turn Claude starts by itself.** In `src/server/agent-runtime/acp/runtime-client.ts:614`, any `tool_call` sets `record.state = "working"`. For turns we didn't prompt, the only thing that sets it back to `idle` is `applyProviderThreadStatus`, and that reads Codex's `threadStatus`. Claude never sends that. Claude marks the end of a turn with a `usage_update` that carries `cost` and `_claude/origin: task-notification`, and nothing reads it. So the 20:47 turn, which made edits, left the worker "working" indefinitely.

2. **The reaper replays your last message whether or not it was already answered.** At `src/server/workers/stuck-worker-reaper.ts:476-481` it always resends the most recent `user_input`. The comment there says "A duplicate response is recoverable." That's wrong for an agent with tools: a replayed "go ahead" counts as consent you never gave. Your message had a complete reply at 20:17:43, so nothing should have been resent.

**What it changed without permission (13 minutes, no commits I could find)**, all in `/Users/masterman/NLP/cloudcaptions`:
- `Cargo.toml`
- a new crate, `rust/crates/cc_face_retouch/` (Cargo.toml, `src/{chunk_codec,identity,settings}.rs`, plus matching tests)
- `rust/crates/face_touchup_core/` (PROVENANCE.md and `src/{frame_plan,observation,params,tracking}.rs`)
- `rust/crates/render_engine/src/{effects_catalog,lib}.rs`
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-02-face-retouch.md`

Other sessions were working in that repo too, so check `git diff` before reverting anything. I haven't touched any of it.

**Proposed fix (not started)**
- **Runtime:** treat a Claude `usage_update` that carries `cost`, when no prompt is in flight, as the end of the turn and set the worker back to idle. This is the same path Codex's `threadStatus: idle` already uses.
- **Reaper:** only resend a message if no turn has completed since it arrived. If the agent already replied, just fix up the worker status and send nothing. I'd also remove the "duplicate response is recoverable" policy.
- **Test:** a lifecycle scenario that reproduces this exact sequence: a prompted turn, then an unprompted turn with tool calls, then the reaper sweep. It should assert that nothing gets resent.

Since the whole problem here was an agent acting without your go-ahead, I'm not changing anything until you say so. Should I implement this fix?

import { mkdir, readFile, realpath, writeFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import { dirname, resolve, sep } from "node:path";
import * as acp from "@agentclientprotocol/sdk";
import { isFullAccessAgentMode } from "../gemini";
import {
  appendBoundedText,
  appendMessageChunk,
  appendOutputEntry,
} from "../output-store";
import { normalizeSessionUpdate } from "./session-updates";
import { TerminalService } from "./terminal-service";
import { McpService, registeredAcpMcpHandlersSnapshot, type AcpMcpHandler } from "./mcp-service";
import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import {
  bufferWorkerPlanStartupUpdate,
  handleAcpSessionUpdateForWorker,
  isAcpPlanNotification,
  type WorkerPlanStartupContext,
} from "./plan-stream";
import {
  handleAcpGoalSessionUpdateForWorker,
  isAcpGoalNotification,
} from "./goal-state";

export type AcpExtensionHandler = {
  request?(params: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<Record<string, unknown>>;
  notify?(params: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<void>;
};
import type {
  AgentRecord,
  ElicitationCreateParams,
  ElicitationResponse,
} from "../types";
import { applyProviderConfigOptions } from "../config-state";

const MAX_TEXT_FIELD_CHARS = 100_000;
const ELICITATION_CREATE_METHOD = "elicitation/create";

/**
 * Human-input request ids are OmniHarness handles, not ACP JSON-RPC ids: they
 * key the pending maps here and every `requestId`-folded row in the worker's
 * append-only stream.
 *
 * That stream outlives this process — a resumed worker keeps writing to the
 * same JSONL — so a counter that restarts at 1 hands a *new* request the id of
 * an old one. Every reader folds by id and lets the later row win, which
 * silently merges two unrelated questions into one card and lets a stale row's
 * terminal status close a live request. Seed from the wall clock so each
 * runtime process starts above every id it could have issued before.
 */
const REQUEST_ID_EPOCH = Date.now();
let nextPermissionRequestId = REQUEST_ID_EPOCH;
let nextElicitationRequestId = REQUEST_ID_EPOCH;

function nowIso() {
  return new Date().toISOString();
}

function finiteNumber(value: unknown) {
  return typeof value === "number" && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null;
}

function asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {
  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value)

    : null;
}

function asNonEmptyString(value: unknown): string | null {
  return typeof value === "string" && value.trim().length > 0 ? value.trim() : null;
}

function updateContextUsage(record: AgentRecord, patch: Partial<NonNullable<AgentRecord["contextUsage"]>>) {
  const existing: NonNullable<AgentRecord["contextUsage"]> = record.contextUsage ?? {
    inputTokens: null,
    outputTokens: null,
    totalTokens: null,
    maxTokens: null,
    fullnessPercent: null,
  };
  const inputTokens = finiteNumber(patch.inputTokens) ?? existing.inputTokens ?? null;
  const outputTokens = finiteNumber(patch.outputTokens) ?? existing.outputTokens ?? null;
  const totalTokens = finiteNumber(patch.totalTokens) ?? existing.totalTokens ?? null;
  const maxTokens = finiteNumber(patch.maxTokens) ?? existing.maxTokens ?? null;
  const explicitFullnessPercent = finiteNumber(patch.fullnessPercent);
  const fullnessPercent = explicitFullnessPercent ?? (
    totalTokens !== null && maxTokens !== null && maxTokens > 0
      ? Math.min(100, Math.max(0, (totalTokens / maxTokens) * 100))
      : existing.fullnessPercent ?? null
  );

  record.contextUsage = {
    inputTokens,
    outputTokens,
    totalTokens,
    maxTokens,
    fullnessPercent,
  };
}


  async sessionUpdate(params: acp.SessionNotification): Promise<void> {

A docs skill your client lists → load it before any docs call — also before a `read`, comment or tab change on a claude.ai …/artifact/… link (the link is a doc; never web-fetch it). No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {\"kind\":\"project\",\"create\":{\"name\":\"<title>\",\"doc\":{\"blocks\":{\"asof\":{\"type\":\"date\",\"value\":\"<today>\"},\"me\":{\"type\":\"mention\",\"user\":\"me\"},\"s1\":{\"type\":\"pending\",\"intent\":\"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to\"},\"s2\":{…}},\"markdown\":\"# <title>\\n\\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\\n\\n<?claude block s1?>\\n\\n<?claude block s2?>\"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. Summoned by a doc comment (turn headed `[Artifact comment sent to Claude]`, `;thread=<root id>`): answer ONLY with a doc comment under that root (`create` an utterance, parent `<root id>`) — no artifact/platform comment tool: that relay thread is resolved and never reaches the doc; an edit asked there → `update` with `answering: \"<root id>\"`."

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:37:06.989Z

I'm checking how a usage_update with cost and the claude/origin metadata signals turn completion, since both prompted and unprompted turns emit cost data—so the real rule is to go idle with end_turn once no prompt is in flight, state is working, and there are no pending permissions or elicitations.

There's no explicit start marker for unprompted turns though—only tool_call sets state to working, not plain message chunks—so I'll keep that minimal rather than inventing a turn-start signal. I'm also verifying that cost-bearing usage_updates only appear at turn boundaries, not mid-turn, by tracing specific timestamps where interrupted or redelivered turns produced their final cost entries.

I want to confirm cost isn't a Claude-specific field other agents might also emit mid-turn, so I should check whether gating purely on promptInFlight is safe or if I need to also check for the `_claude/origin` metadata alongside the cost field to avoid false positives from other providers like codex or gemini.

                            // A resumed session's first reading has no predecessor and

                                if (message.state === "running" &&

                                    session.owedTrailingIdles > 0) {
                                    // A transition INTO `running` (the CLI reports state
                                    // changes; a repeated `running` is not a transition and is
                                    // left alone) proves the idle period before it ended: every
                                    // idle the CLI was going to emit for earlier results has
                                    // been emitted. Debt still outstanding here can never be paid.
                                    // CLI 2.1.270+ withholds `idle` while background agents run
                                    // (verified live: user result → task_notification →
                                    // followup result → ONE idle), so a held turn's own result
                                    // leaves one unpaid unit per hold. Left in place, each unit
                                    // would absorb a later un-owed idle — masking an issue-#825
                                    // detection — or swallow the idle a steered turn settles on
                                    // (the owed-idle branch below runs before the steer lane),
                                    // hanging that prompt. Sweep it.
                                    this.logger.log(`[claude-agent-acp] Session ${params.sessionId}: dropping ${session.owedTrailingIdles} unpaid trailing-idle debt at the running transition`);
                                    session.owedTrailingIdles = 0;
                                }
                                if (message.state === "idle") {
                                    // A non-cancelled turn normally settled at its terminal
                                    // `result` already (issue #773), and that result recorded an
                                    // owed trailing idle — absorbed here via the decrement. We
                                    // must NOT settle `activeTurn` on an owed idle: `idle`
                                    // carries no turn identity, and it can lag (the SDK flushes
                                    // held-back results / drains background agents first), so by
                                    // the time it arrives the SDK may have echoed the NEXT turn
                                    // and activated it — settling now would resolve that new

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:39:47.022Z

I'm checking whether the adapter actually forwards session_state_changed events to ACP clients, but the canonical stream doesn't show one, so the real signal must be the cost-bearing usage_update instead. I'm leaning toward treating a usage_update as the trigger only when there's no prompt in flight and no pending human input.

I considered also treating an unprompted agent message chunk while idle as a trigger symmetric to tool_call, but that risks flipping state to "working" with no cost update to follow, leaving it stuck and vulnerable to reaper cancellation — so I'll keep the detection minimal and skip text-start triggers.

For the DB status discrepancy, I need to check how DB worker status syncs with bridge state and whether polling or the sessionUpdate timestamp ordering explains why liveAgentFinishedAfterLastEntry evaluates false, which would misroute into the redeliver path and directly re-ask — this is the actual reaper bug fix 2 needs to address by determining whether the last user_input was truly answered via some turn completion marker in the stream.

Looking at the execution events timeline, I'm trying to match timestamps against auto_commit_skipped entries to see if a turn for the 20:16 message was somehow held open until 20:33:54, possibly because the adapter deferred settlement for background subagents before resolving the prompt response.

Now I'm wondering how the reaper actually determines a user message got answered — whether it checks the workers table for a last-completed-turn timestamp, or inspects the messages table directly for persisted assistant responses alongside user checkpoints.

Other options include scanning the stream for a provider-specific usage/cost entry, or relying on execution events like "auto_commit_skipped" emitted at turn end — though that feels hacky. I think it's better to trace the send-message completion path itself, looking at functions like appendAskResponseFallbackEntry and updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput to see what gets recorded when a turn finishes.

CREATE TABLE workers (
  id text PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL,
  run_id text NOT NULL,
  type text NOT NULL,
  status text NOT NULL,
  cwd text NOT NULL,
  created_at integer NOT NULL,
  updated_at integer NOT NULL, output_log text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', bridge_session_id text, bridge_session_mode text, output_entries_json text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', current_text text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', last_text text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', title text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', initial_prompt text NOT NULL DEFAULT '', worker_number integer, active_work_started_at integer, active_work_duration_ms integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, worker_role text, allocation_key text, turn_generation integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, effective_launch_model text, effective_launch_effort text, launch_credential_source text, superseded_from_seq integer, superseded_seq_ranges text, launch_selection_revision integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  FOREIGN KEY (run_id) REFERENCES runs(id) ON UPDATE no action ON DELETE no action
);
CREATE INDEX workers_run_idx ON workers(run_id);
CREATE TRIGGER workers_work_timer_insert
AFTER INSERT ON workers
WHEN lower(substr(NEW.status, 1, instr(NEW.status || ':', ':') - 1)) = 'working'
BEGIN
  UPDATE workers
  SET active_work_started_at = COALESCE(NEW.active_work_started_at, NEW.created_at),
      active_work_duration_ms = COALESCE(NEW.active_work_duration_ms, 0)
  WHERE id = NEW.id;
END;
CREATE TRIGGER workers_work_timer_start
AFTER UPDATE OF status, updated_at ON workers
WHEN lower(substr(OLD.status, 1, instr(OLD.status || ':', ':') - 1)) != 'working'
  AND lower(substr(NEW.status, 1, instr(NEW.status || ':', ':') - 1)) = 'working'
BEGIN
  UPDATE workers
  SET active_work_started_at = NEW.updated_at,
      active_work_duration_ms = COALESCE(NEW.active_work_duration_ms, OLD.active_work_duration_ms, 0)
  WHERE id = NEW.id;
END;
CREATE TRIGGER workers_work_timer_stop
AFTER UPDATE OF status, updated_at ON workers
WHEN lower(substr(OLD.status, 1, instr(OLD.status || ':', ':') - 1)) = 'working'
  AND lower(substr(NEW.status, 1, instr(NEW.status || ':', ':') - 1)) != 'working'
BEGIN
  UPDATE workers
  SET active_work_started_at = NULL,
      active_work_duration_ms = COALESCE(OLD.active_work_duration_ms, 0)
        + (MAX(0, NEW.updated_at - COALESCE(OLD.active_work_started_at, OLD.updated_at, OLD.created_at, NEW.updated_at)) * 1000)
  WHERE id = NEW.id;
END;
CREATE INDEX workers_run_created_id_idx ON workers(run_id, created_at, id);
CREATE INDEX workers_created_id_idx ON workers(created_at, id);
CREATE TRIGGER runs_activity_on_worker_turn_end
AFTER UPDATE OF status ON workers
WHEN lower(substr(OLD.status, 1, instr(OLD.status || ':', ':') - 1)) = 'working'
  AND lower(substr(NEW.status, 1, instr(NEW.status || ':', ':') - 1)) != 'working'
BEGIN
  UPDATE runs
  SET last_activity_at = NEW.updated_at
  WHERE id = NEW.run_id
    AND COALESCE(last_activity_at, 0) < NEW.updated_at;
END;
CREATE TRIGGER workers_direct_cli_invariant
BEFORE INSERT ON workers
WHEN EXISTS (
  SELECT 1
  FROM runs
  WHERE runs.id = NEW.run_id
    AND runs.session_type = 'omni'
    AND runs.mode IN ('direct', 'commit')
)
AND EXISTS (
  SELECT 1
  FROM workers existing
  WHERE existing.run_id = NEW.run_id
    AND CASE replace(replace(replace(lower(trim(existing.type)), '-', ''), '_', ''), ' ', '')
      WHEN 'codexcli' THEN 'codex'
      WHEN 'codexacp' THEN 'codex'
      WHEN 'claudecode' THEN 'claude'
      WHEN 'geminicli' THEN 'gemini'
      ELSE replace(replace(replace(lower(trim(existing.type)), '-', ''), '_', ''), ' ', '')
    END <> CASE replace(replace(replace(lower(trim(NEW.type)), '-', ''), '_', ''), ' ', '')
      WHEN 'codexcli' THEN 'codex'
      WHEN 'codexacp' THEN 'codex'
      WHEN 'claudecode' THEN 'claude'
      WHEN 'geminicli' THEN 'gemini'
      ELSE replace(replace(replace(lower(trim(NEW.type)), '-', ''), '_', ''), ' ', '')
    END
)
BEGIN
  SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'HANDOFF_REQUIRED');
END;

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:40:06.113Z

I'm thinking about whether just checking for any agent output after the user_input in seq order is a reliable signal, but the reaper's comment flags this as fragile since buffered output from a prior turn can land with earlier timestamps despite later seq numbers—and indeed this stream shows exactly that pattern with the user_input and session_info entries.

The fix might be requiring timestamp >= userInputTs alongside seq > userInputSeq, since real responses have later timestamps while buffered entries don't. But I'm also considering the actual failure mode the reaper is meant to catch: if the agent hangs mid-turn after already producing output, redelivering the user message seems wrong—that re-runs the request rather than resuming it, so maybe the right recovery there is a continuation prompt like INTERRUPTED_DIRECT_TURN_PROMPT rather than replaying the original message. I'm sketching out a policy: redeliver only when there's truly no agent output after the user_input, and otherwise check if the live agent is quiesced before deciding how to resume.

Now I'm worried that sending a "continue" prompt to a worker that actually already finished the task could cause it to start something new entirely, like kicking off an unrelated Task 0 — that's dangerous. So the safer rule might be: once the user message has been answered, never inject any further prompt, and only consider cancelling a hung turn if the bridge genuinely reports "working" — though even that's risky since a legitimate long-running tool call (like a 10-minute test) might look stalled while actually progressing.

Actually, I should skip redelivery entirely and just record an execution event like `recovery_redelivery_skipped` with reason "already_answered", setting status from the live agent. I need to check the named events union types to see if I should add a new kind, since the lifecycle doc requires typed named events for decisions — let me check that doc and existing reaper tests.

180	    const TEN_MIN_AGO = new Date(Date.now() - 10 * 60_000);
181	    const { runId, workerId } = await setupRun({
182	      mode: "direct",
183	      workerStatus: "working",
184	      workerUpdatedAt: TEN_MIN_AGO,
185	    });
186	
187	    const userMessageId = randomUUID();
188	    await db.insert(messages).values({
189	      id: userMessageId,
190	      runId,
191	      role: "user",
192	      kind: "checkpoint",
193	      content: "continue",
194	      createdAt: TEN_MIN_AGO,
195	    });
196	    await writeUserInputEntry(runId, workerId, {
197	      id: userMessageId,
198	      text: "continue",
199	      timestamp: TEN_MIN_AGO,
200	    });
201	
202	    mockCancelAgent.mockResolvedValue({ ok: true });
203	    mockResumeMissingDirectWorker.mockImplementation(async () => {
204	      // Reproduce the real resume result from 054dc5ddea78: the recovered
205	      // bridge agent is idle, so reconciliation temporarily settles both rows
206	      // before the reaper re-delivers the interrupted prompt.
207	      await db.update(workers).set({ status: "idle" }).where(eq(workers.id, workerId));
208	      await db.update(runs).set({ status: "done" }).where(eq(runs.id, runId));
209	      return { name: workerId, state: "idle" };
210	    });
211	
212	    let resolveAsk!: (value: { response: string; state: string }) => void;
213	    let markAskStarted!: () => void;
214	    const askStarted = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
215	      markAskStarted = resolve;
216	    });
217	    const askResult = new Promise<{ response: string; state: string }>((resolve) => {
218	      resolveAsk = resolve;
219	    });
220	    mockAskAgent.mockImplementation(async () => {
221	      markAskStarted();
222	      return askResult;
223	    });
224	
225	    const sweep = reapStuckDirectWorkers();
226	    await askStarted;
227	    const workerWhileAsking = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();
228	    const runWhileAsking = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();
229	    const eventKindsWhileAsking = getNamedEventsSince(null, { runId })
230	      .events
231	      .map((entry) => entry.event.kind);
232	
233	    resolveAsk({ response: "finished", state: "idle" });
234	    await sweep;
235	
236	    expect(workerWhileAsking?.status).toBe("working");
237	    expect(runWhileAsking?.status).toBe("running");
238	    expect(eventKindsWhileAsking).toContain("worker.recovery_continuation_started");
239	  });

494	    expect(rows.at(-1)?.status).toBe("cancelled");

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:40:29.610Z

I'm figuring out how to detect whether a user_input was "answered" by checking if an agent-authored entry type—like message, thought, tool_call, or tool_call_update—appears later in the stream.

For the genuine mid-turn hang case, I'm weighing whether cancel/resume without redelivering the user message is too passive: the user would just see the turn stop and have to say "continue." That feels consistent with the conservative design priorities, but I should decide whether to emit a named event or an error code like something resembling "worker.turn.interrupted" so the interruption is visible rather than silent.

Looking at the existing reaper test "reaps again once the question has been answered," the stream has elicitation pending and answered at the same timestamp as user_input. Elicitation technically counts as the agent responding, so if I treat elicitation/permission as agent responses, this scenario shifts to no-redelivery, meaning I'd need to update that test to still assert cancellation but drop the expectation that it re-asks, since replaying an already-answered message violates the new no-replay policy.

For the "re-asks a live idle agent directly when the prompt was dropped" test, the stream only has user_input with no answer, so redelivery should still happen there — that one stays unchanged, as does the lost-completion reconciliation test.

Now I'm working out the reaper's branching logic for the answered case: when the live agent has quiesced, I want to reuse the existing lost-completion path by broadening its condition to also trigger when the agent is quiesced and the last user input was answered, treating it like an unprompted-turn completion. For the case where the bridge still reports the agent as working (not quiesced), the flow falls into cancel-and-resume without redelivery, and I need to figure out what status to set afterward since that no-redeliver branch currently only handles the no-user_input case.

I want to check whether resumeMissingDirectWorker itself updates the status to idle, since the mocks in the tests seem to assume that happens.

Now I'll check what status `resumeMissingDirectWorker` leaves things in.

```text
export async function resumeMissingDirectWorker(
  run: RunRecord,
  worker: WorkerRecord,
  expectedTurnGeneration?: number,
) {
  const workerPredicate = expectedTurnGeneration === undefined
    ? eq(workers.id, worker.id)
    : and(
      eq(workers.id, worker.id),
      eq(workers.turnGeneration, expectedTurnGeneration),
    );
  let sessionId = worker.bridgeSessionId?.trim();
  let sessionMode = worker.bridgeSessionMode?.trim();
  if (!sessionId) {
    const metadata = await readWorkerSessionMetadata(worker.runId, worker.id);
    if (metadata) {
      sessionId = metadata.sessionId;
      sessionMode = metadata.sessionMode ?? sessionMode;
      await db.update(workers).set({
        bridgeSessionId: metadata.sessionId,
        bridgeSessionMode: metadata.sessionMode ?? worker.bridgeSessionMode,
        updatedAt: new Date(),
      }).where(workerPredicate);
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "worker.session_metadata_repaired",
        runId: worker.runId,
        workerId: worker.id,
      });
    }
  }
  // Clones have a durable transcript and an idle worker row, but no runtime
  // session until the user sends their first message.
  const unstartedFork = !sessionId && Boolean(run.parentRunId);
  if (!sessionId && !unstartedFork) {
    const message = `Direct worker ${worker.id} is missing persisted ACP session metadata.`;
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "error.surfaced",
      code: "worker.resume.failed",
      message,
      surface: "banner",
      runId: run.id,
      workerId: worker.id,
      cause: null,
    });
    throw Object.assign(new Error(message), { status: 500 });
  }

  const yoloModeEnabled = await readWorkerYoloModeEnabled();
  const workerMode = resolveWorkerLaunchMode(sessionMode, yoloModeEnabled);
  const { env: envParams } = await readRuntimeEnvFromSettings();
  const initialSelection = resolveWorkerLaunchSelection(worker, run);
  const forkAccount = unstartedFork && initialSelection.credentialSource === "account"
    ? await allocateWorkerAccount({
      workerType: worker.type,
      runId: run.id,
      workerId: worker.id,
      explicitAccountId: run.preferredWorkerAccountId,
      strategy: run.preferredWorkerAccountId ? "manual" : "priority",
      env: envParams,
    })
    : null;
  const launchSelection = resolveWorkerLaunchSelection(worker, run, {
    accountId: forkAccount?.account?.id ?? await readWorkerAllocatedAccountId(worker.id),
  });
  const spawnParams = {
    type: worker.type,
    cwd: worker.cwd,
    name: worker.id,
    ...(workerMode ? { mode: workerMode } : {}),
    env: envParams,
    ...(launchSelection.accountId ? { accountId: launchSelection.accountId } : {}),
    ...(launchSelection.model ? { model: launchSelection.model } : {}),
    ...(launchSelection.effort ? { effort: launchSelection.effort } : {}),

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:40:51.594Z

I'm adding logic to the session update handler so that when a usage update signals a turn result, it settles any unprompted turn—bailing out early if a prompt is still in flight or the provider's turn is still active.

I'm also refactoring the idle branch of applyProviderThreadStatus into a shared endProviderTurn function, and writing a helper that checks whether a usage update represents a final cost result versus an intermediate one, with a note about why claude-agent-acp only sends cost on completed results. I'm thinking through an edge case with placeholder zero-turn results and whether lastText gets properly updated when the bridge is idle versus working, then checking how the emitted provider_turn_ended event propagates to keep the DB worker status in sync.

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:41:31.297Z

Setting that background search aside, I'm moving forward with the reaper fix the user authorized. I'm tracking whether the last user input got answered by resetting a flag on each new user_input and marking it true once a later agent-response entry appears, then using that to detect lost completions.

For the redelivery decision, I'm setting shouldRedeliver only when there's a pending unanswered user input, and handling the case where it was answered but we're not redelivering by recording a skip event and syncing the worker status from the resume result instead of leaving it stuck.

I should also name a new recovery event for this skipped-redelivery case, and think about whether to surface this to the user as a visible interruption notice since their turn was effectively cancelled. Let me check whether named events are tracked in a separate registry beyond the type union by searching for similar existing event kinds across the codebase.



uuid: 7a3f9192-78b4-4924-915d-46c201c87a92
parent: ba54c35f-033c-482c-ab5b-b46bf6b5cca9

## Claude thinking · 2026-10-02T23:42:31.030Z

Those errors are pre-existing in old test lines I didn't touch, so I'll leave them alone since the source files typecheck clean. I'm checking that resumeMissingDirectWorker's return type correctly matches AgentRecord and verifying whether updateDirectRunStatusFromWorkerOutput properly accepts the rendered output parameter.

Now shifting to the reaper tests: I need to update "reaps again once the question has been answered" since the elicitation timestamps now match, meaning it's answered and shouldn't trigger an ask. I'll also add new test cases covering an incident repro where user input precedes agent activity with a live idle bridge, a stale-bridge scenario needing cancel and resume with redelivery skip, and a buffered prior-turn output case that should still get redelivered. I'm also noting that tests share a DB across the file so I need to make sure my tests leave the worker idle at the end, and I'm planning to add a lifecycle scenario file mirroring the bug per the project's testing conventions before writing the actual unit tests.

  "content": "/**\n * Reproduces session 3d628f9b568a: the user's \"yes go ahead with plan\" was\n * answered, then Claude ran a turn on its own after a background-task\n * notification and finished it with \"Say the word and I'll start on Task 0.\"\n * The runtime never reported that unprompted turn as idle, so five minutes\n * later the stuck-worker reaper cancelled the session, resumed it, and\n * re-sent the answered message. The agent read the replay as fresh consent and\n * started implementing.\n *\n * The rule: a message the agent already responded to is never re-sent.\n */\nimport { randomUUID } from \"crypto\";\nimport { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\nimport { eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { db } from \"@/server/db\";\nimport { artifactStreams, __resetOutputStoreCachesForTests,\n  writeWorkerOutputEntries,\n} from \"@/server/workers/output-store\";\nimport { clearLifecycleSchema } from \"../harness/fixtures\";\n\nconst { mockAskAgent, mockCancelAgent, mockGetAgent } = vi.hoisted(() => ({\n  mockAskAgent: vi.fn(),\n  mockCancelAgent: vi.fn(),\n  mockGetAgent: vi.fn(),\n}));\n\nvi.mock(\"@/server/bridge-client\", async    getAgent: mockGetAgent,\n  };\n});\n\nconst { mockResumeMissingDirectWorker } = vi.hoisted(() => ({\n  mockResumeMissingDirectWorker: vi.fn(),\n}));\n\nvi.mock(\"@/server/conversations/send-message\", async (importOriginal) => {\n  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import(\"@/server/conversations/send-message\")>();\n  return {\n    ...actual,\n    resumeMissingDirectWorker: mockResumeMissingDirectWorker,\n  };\n});\n\nimport { reapStuckDirectWorkers } from \"@/server/workers/stuck-worker-reaper\";\n\nbeforeEach(async () => {\n  __resetNamedEventsForTests();\n  __resetOutputStoreCachesForTests();\n  mockAskAgent.mockReset();\n  mockCancelAgent.mockReset();\n  mockGetAgent.mockReset();\n  mockResumeMissingDirectWorker.mockReset();\n  await db.delete(executionEvents);\n  await db.delete(artifactStreams);\n  await db.delete(messages);\n  await clearLifecycleSchema();\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  await db.delete(executionEvents);\n  await db.delete(artifactStreams);\n  await db.delete(messages);\n  await clearLifecycleSchema();\n});\n\nasync function seedAnsweredConversation() {\n  const planId = `plan-${randomUUID()}`;\n  const runId = `run-${randomUUID()}`;\n  const workerId = `${runId}-worker-1`;\n  const userMessageId = randomUUID();\n  const askedAt = new Date(Date.now() - 36 * 60_000);\n  const lastTurnEndedAt = new Date(Date.now() - 6 * 60_000);\n  const now = new Date();\n\n  await db.insert(plans).values({\n    id: planId,\n    path: `/tmp/${planId}.md`,\n    status: \"running\",\n    createdAt: now,\n    updatedAt: now,\n  });\n  await db.insert(runs).values({\n    id: runId,\n    planId,\n    mode: \"direct\",\n    status: \"running\",\n    createdAt: now,\n    updatedAt: now,\n  });\n  await db.insert(workers).values({\n    id: workerId,\n    runId,\n    type: \"claude\",\n    cwd: \"/tmp\",\n    status: \"working\",\n    workerNumber: 1,\n    bridgeSessionId: \"session-3d628f9b568a\",\n    createdAt: now,\n    updatedAt: lastTurnEndedAt,\n  });\n  await db.insert(messages).values({\n    id: userMessageId,\n    runId,\n    role: \"user\",\n    kind: \"checkpoint\",\n    content: \"web too why not, as long as it works\\n\\nv2 first\\n\\nyes go ahead with plan\",\n    createdAt: askedAt,\n  });\n  await writeWorkerOutputEntries(runId, workerId, [\n    {\n      id: userMessageId,\n      type: \"user_input\",\n      text: \"web too why not, as long as it works\\n\\nv2 first\\n\\nyes go ahead with plan\",\n      timestamp: askedAt.toISOString(),\n      authorRole: \"user\",\n      channel: \"stdin\",\n      seq: 1,\n    },\n    {\n      id: randomUUID(),\n      type: \"message\",\n      text: \"Launching the research agents in the background.\",\n      timestamp: new Date(askedAt.getTime() + 60_000).toISOString(),\n      seq: 2,\n    },\n    // The unprompted task-notification turn: tools, then its closing text.\n    {\n      id: randomUUID(),\n      type: \"tool_call\",\n      text: \"Edit\",\n      status: \"pending\",\n      timestamp: new Date(lastTurnEndedAt.getTime() - 20_000).toISOString(),\n      seq: 3,\n    },\n    {\n      id: randomUUID(),\n      type: \"message\",\n      text: \"Say the word and I'll start on Task 0.\",\n      timestamp: lastTurnEndedAt.toISOString(),\n      seq: 4,\n    },\n  ]);\n\n  return { runId, workerId, lastTurnEndedAt };\n}\n\nfunction claudeAgent(workerId: string, state: string, updatedAt: Date) {\n  return {\n    name: workerId,\n    type: \"claude\",\n    cwd: \"/tmp\",\n    state,\n    stopReason: state === \"idle\" ? \"end_turn\" : null,\n    currentText: \"\",\n    lastText: \"Say the word and I'll start on Task 0.\",\n    stderrBuffer: [],\n    outputEntries: [],\n    pendingPermissions: [],\n    pendingElicitations: [],\n    updatedAt: updatedAt.toISOString(),\n  };\n}\n\ndescribe(\"lifecycle — stuck direct-worker reaper and an answered message\", () => {\n  it(\"settles the conversation without re-sending once the unprompted turn has ended\", async () => {\n    const { runId, workerId, lastTurnEndedAt } = await seedAnsweredConversation();\n    mockGetAgent.mockResolvedValue(claudeAgent(workerId, \"idle\", lastTurnEndedAt));\n\n    await expect(reapStuckDirectWorkers()).resolves.toMatchObject({ ok: true });\n\n    expect(mockAskAgent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(mockCancelAgent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(mockResumeMissingDirectWorker).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    const worker = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();\n    const run = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();\n    expect(worker?.status).toBe(\"idle\");\n    expect(run?.status).toBe(\"done\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"never re-sends the answered message even when the runtime still claims the turn is running\", async () => {\n    const { runId, workerId, lastTurnEndedAt } = await seedAnsweredConversation();\n    mockGetAgent.mockResolvedValue(claudeAgent(workerId, \"working\", lastTurnEndedAt));\n    mockCancelAgent.mockResolvedValue({ ok: true });\n    mockResumeMissingDirectWorker.mockResolvedValue({ name: workerId, state: \"idle\" });\n\n    await expect(reapStuckDirectWorkers()).resolves.toMatchObject({ ok: true });\n\n    expect(mockAskAgent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    const worker = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();\n    expect(worker?.status).toBe(\"idle\");\n    const events = getNamedEventsSince(null, { runId }).events.map((entry) => entry.event);\n    expect(events).toContainEqual(expect.objectContaining({\n      kind: \"worker.recovery_redelivery_skipped\",\n      runId,\n      workerId,\n      reason: \"already_answered\",\n    }));\n    expect(events.map((event) => event.kind)).not.toContain(\"worker.recovery_continuation_started\");\n  });\n});\n"
}

## Claude · 2026-10-02T23:46:24.714Z

I fixed both halves of the bug, and the tests pass. The fix only takes effect once OmniHarness restarts. I haven't restarted it because that would interrupt any sessions running right now.

**Fix 1: the worker now goes idle after a turn Claude starts by itself** (`src/server/agent-runtime/acp/runtime-client.ts`)
The Claude adapter (`claude-agent-acp` 0.81.0) attaches `cost` to a `usage_update` only when a turn ends. That includes the turns it runs on its own after a background task finishes. When one of those arrives and OmniHarness didn't send the prompt, the runtime now marks the turn idle. It skips this while a prompt from OmniHarness is in flight, while a Codex `/goal` turn is running, or while a permission or question is waiting on you.

**Fix 2: the reaper never resends a message the agent already answered** (`src/server/workers/stuck-worker-reaper.ts`)
- A message counts as answered if the agent wrote any output after it (text, thinking, tool calls, permission requests or questions) with a timestamp no earlier than the message. Leftover output from the previous turn has an earlier timestamp, so it doesn't count.
- **Answered, and the agent is idle:** the reaper only updates the status. It cancels nothing, restarts nothing and sends nothing. This is the path your session will take from now on.
- **Answered, but the agent still claims to be working:** the reaper stops and resumes the session as before but sends nothing. It then logs a `recovery_redelivery_skipped` event, so you can see the turn was stopped.
- A message the agent never responded to, meaning it was lost on the way, is still resent as before.
- I removed the comment that said "a duplicate response is recoverable."

**Behavior change in one existing test:** previously, if the agent had asked you a question, you answered, and the worker then went quiet, the reaper resent your original message. Now it only stops the hung turn. I updated "reaps again once the question has been answered" to expect that.

**Tests**
- New runtime tests:
  - a background-task turn ends as idle
  - a usage update without `cost` doesn't end the turn
  - a turn OmniHarness started is left alone
  - a pending permission keeps the turn working
- New reaper tests that replay session 3d628f9b568a. Against the original reaper code, the new tests fail along with the updated existing one, 3 failures in all. With the fix, all 19 reaper tests pass.
- New lifecycle scenario: `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/stuck-direct-worker-answered-message.test.ts`.
- Lint is clean. `tsc` reports no errors in the changed source files. The three `providerTurnActive` type errors in `runtime-client.test.ts` are on older lines I didn't touch.
- Full runs:
  - `pnpm test:lifecycle`: 66/67 passed
  - related server suites: 451/453 passed
  - The three failures were the plan-widget lifecycle test and two Claude model-pinning tests. Both files pass on their own with these changes, so the failures look like timing flakes under load.

I haven't committed anything. Your existing uncommitted changes, like the locales and `GoalPlanCard`, are untouched.
